    Outside capture this executes immediately, so the same call works eagerly.

    :param alpha: Prefactor on op(A_i) op(B_i), shared by the whole call.
    :param a_list: Left operands, all rank 2. Shapes may differ between members.
    :param b_list: Right operands, same length as ``a_list``.
    :param beta: Prefactor on C_i. Non-zero means every C_i is read as well as written, which the node records as a dependency.
    :param c_list: Destinations, same length as ``a_list``. Must be distinct tensors: the call gives no ordering between members, so two members sharing a destination race.
    :param trans_a: Transpose each A_i.
    :param trans_b: Transpose each B_i.


.. py:function:: grouped_batched_gemm_blocked(alpha: float, a_list: list[Any], b_list: list[Any], beta: float, c_bases: list[Any], c_offsets: list[int], trans_a: bool = False, trans_b: bool = False) -> None

    ``grouped_batched_gemm`` where the destinations are blocks of existing
    tensors, described by a base and an offset rather than enumerated as views.

    The blocked form of ``batched_gemm`` exists because materializing one view
    per destination is tens of thousands of pybind round trips in a local
    correlation method. The grouped form exists because one OpenMP region per
    shape class costs more than the arithmetic. A caller whose destinations are
    blocks AND whose shapes differ between classes needs both, and the DLPNO
    overlap build is exactly that caller: one destination tensor per shape
    class, every coupling a column range of its class's tensor.

    So ``c_bases`` is parallel to the members, not to the groups. Repeating a
    base pointer across a class's members is a pointer copy; it is constructing
    the views that is expensive, and none are constructed. Each block's shape is
    derived from its own A and B rather than passed, and its leading dimension
    from its base, which is what makes a column range expressible this way.

    The node declares each DISTINCT base as an output, so a later read of a
    whole base is ordered against this write. The list form declares the views,
    and the graph does not know a view write touches its parent.

    :param alpha: Prefactor on op(A_i) op(B_i), shared by the whole call.
    :param a_list: Left operands, all rank 2. Shapes may differ between members.
    :param b_list: Right operands, same length as ``a_list``.
    :param beta: Prefactor on C_i. Non-zero means every block is read as well as written, which the node records as a dependency.
    :param c_bases: Per member, the tensor its block lives in.
    :param c_offsets: Per member, the block's first element as an offset into its base, in elements.
    :param trans_a: Transpose each A_i.
    :param trans_b: Transpose each B_i.


.. py:function:: last_dispatch_route_name() -> str

    The kernel route the most recent contraction on this thread took, as a string.

    A copy of ``last_dispatch_route`` for the Python bindings, which cannot hold the
    thread-local slot itself. It serves the same purpose: a test asserts that a spec reached its
    fast path, since a fallback to the generic loop still computes the right values and only the
    route shows it. Read it on the thread that ran the contraction, straight after the call.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :returns: The route name, or ``"none"`` if this thread has run no contraction.


.. py:function:: load_graph_file(path: str) -> Graph

    Read ``path`` back as a runnable graph, throwing on failure.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param path: The file to read.
    :returns: The graph. The caller owns it; Python's reference counting takes it from here.
    :raises Exception: With the failure's message.


.. py:function:: load_graph_file_into(path: str, registry: SpaceRegistry) -> Graph

    Read ``path`` back against ``registry``, throwing on failure. The Python spelling.

    The counterpart of the ``load_graph`` overload taking a ``SpaceRegistry``, for a caller
    who keeps their own registry rather than using the process-global one.

    The overload is named in prose rather than written out as a signature: a cross-reference
    followed immediately by a parameter list renders as an inline literal with an unterminated
    start-string, and the docs build treats that warning as an error.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param path: The file to read.
    :param registry: The registry the file's space NAMES resolve in. Must outlive the graph.
    :returns: The graph, owned by the caller.
    :raises Exception: With every problem the file has.


.. py:function:: make_approximation_record(pass_name: str, effect: ApproximationEffect, tolerance: float, bound: float, outputs: list[str] = std::vector<std::string>{}, spaces: list[str] = std::vector<std::string>{}, setup: str = "", origin: ApproximationOrigin = einsums::compute_graph::ApproximationOrigin::Asserted, measurement: str = "") -> ApproximationRecord

    Build an ``ApproximationRecord`` in one call.

    A free function rather than a constructor, so ``ApproximationRecord`` stays an AGGREGATE
    and C++ callers keep designated initializers, which is how a struct of seven fields stays
    readable at a call site. Declaring even a defaulted constructor would take that away.
    Python has no aggregate initialization and needs some entry point, and this is a better
    one than a default-constructed record with seven assignments after it.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param pass_name: The pass applying the rewrite.
    :param effect: What ``bound`` is a bound on.
    :param tolerance: The knob the pass was asked for.
    :param bound: The effect the pass states it had.
    :param outputs: Manifest names the bound applies to; empty means all of them.
    :param spaces: Index-space names the rewrite involved.
    :param setup: Label of the setup node it created, or empty.
    :param origin: Whether ``bound`` was computed or claimed.
    :param measurement: Name of the tensor carrying the measured error per bind, or empty.
    :returns: The record.


.. py:function:: make_index_space(name: str, scale_symbol: str, typical_extent: float = 0., growth: GrowthClass = einsums::compute_graph::GrowthClass::linear(), dim_symbol: str = std::string{}) -> IndexSpace

    Build an ``IndexSpace`` from its parts.

    For callers without aggregate initialization (Python).

    :param name: Human-readable name, unique within a registry.
    :param scale_symbol: Letter this space contributes to a symbolic cost polynomial.
    :param typical_extent: Advisory extent. Zero, the default, means unset.
    :param growth: How the extent grows with system size. Linear by default.
    :param dim_symbol: Name a symbolic extent over this space goes by ("no", "nv", ...). Empty, the default, when the space has none.
    :returns: The space.


.. py:function:: make_provenance_tag(name: str, attributes: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> ProvenanceTag

    Build a ``ProvenanceTag``.

    A named constructor rather than a real one, for the same reason ``make_index_space`` is one:
    ``ProvenanceTag`` stays an aggregate so the library's designated-initializer construction
    sites keep working, and this is what a caller with no aggregate initialization builds one
    through.

    Both parameters are required rather than the second defaulted, which is a binding constraint
    rather than a preference: the codegen renders a ``= {}`` default as a bare brace-init that
    pybind's ``py::arg`` cannot be assigned from. The Python-side helper in ``einsums.graph``
    supplies the empty list, so a caller there still writes just the name.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param name: The vocabulary name.
    :param attributes: Key/value pairs, possibly empty. Sorted by ``Graph::annotate_tag`` on the way in, so the order given here does not matter.
    :returns: The tag.


.. py:function:: permute_view(parent: Any, perm: list[int]) -> RuntimeTensorView

    Python-friendly transpose / axis-permutation view.

    Records a graph View whose result axis ``i`` aliases parent axis
    ``perm[i]`` (``perm`` must be a permutation of ``[0, rank)``). The returned
    view is graph-owned and aliases the parent. Unlike the raw
    ``RuntimeTensor.transpose_view()``, which shares the parent's data pointer
    and is invisible to the graph, a transposed tensor here can be fed to captured
    gemm/axpy/etc. ops. This is what backs the capture-aware ``A.T``.

    .. code-block:: text

       with cg.capture(g):
             At = einsums.graph.permute_view(A, [1, 0])   # A.T, graph-registered
             einsums.linalg.gemm(1.0, At, B, 0.0, C)


.. py:function:: plan_nary_einsum(spec: str, dims: list[list[int]], max_intermediate_elements: int = 0) -> list[NaryEinsumStep]

    The cheapest order to contract an einsum over several operands two at a time.

    The order is the binary tree whose arithmetic, priced at these extents, is least: the same
    search and the same ranking the graph's ``MultiTermFactorization`` uses on a captured product.
    Each partial product keeps only the letters a later step or the output still needs, so an index
    shared by the first two operands alone is summed when they are contracted.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param spec: The spec, three or more operands; two are planned too, as one step.
    :param dims: Each operand's extents, in the spec's order.
    :param max_intermediate_elements: The most elements a partial product may hold, or zero for no limit. When no order keeps every partial product within it, the limit is dropped and the cheapest order is returned.
    :returns: The steps, in the order to run them.
    :raises Exception: when the spec is malformed, names a different number of operands than ``dims`` holds, gives an operand the wrong rank, or a letter two different extents.


.. py:function:: plan_nary_einsum_with_spaces(graph: Graph, spec: str, dims: list[list[int]], spaces: list[list[SpaceId]], max_intermediate_elements: int = 0) -> list[NaryEinsumStep]

    The cheapest order to contract an einsum's operands, ranked by the index spaces they are annotated with.

    ``plan_nary_einsum`` with each letter bound to the space its axes are annotated with, by the rules a
    two-operand einsum binds its letters at capture, and ranked by the scaling ``graph's`` registry
    declares: an order that is cheaper asymptotically wins even where these extents would favour
    another, as ``MultiTermFactorization`` ranks it. A letter no annotated axis carries is
    anonymous and ranked by its extent.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param graph: The graph whose space registry the annotations belong to.
    :param spec: The spec, three or more operands; two are planned too, as one step.
    :param dims: Each operand's extents, in the spec's order.
    :param spaces: Each operand's per-axis spaces in the spec's order, then C's, each empty where the tensor is not annotated; or empty altogether.
    :param max_intermediate_elements: As for ``plan_nary_einsum``.
    :returns: The steps, in the order to run them.
    :raises Exception: as ``plan_nary_einsum`` does, when ``spaces`` does not hold one entry per operand and one for C, or when one letter is annotated with two different spaces.


.. py:function:: save_graph_file(graph: Graph, path: str, allow_machine_dependent: bool = False) -> None

    Write ``graph`` to ``path``, throwing on refusal.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param graph: The graph to write.
    :param path: Destination file.
    :param allow_machine_dependent: Write the graph even when a structural-resource or tuning pass has changed it; see ``SaveOptions::allow_machine_dependent``.
    :raises Exception: With the refusal's message.


.. py:function:: stranded_materializations(graph: Graph) -> list[str]

    Graph-owned intermediates that are materialized and that nothing reads or writes.

    The counterpart of ``Materialization::num_unused``, asked of a finished graph rather than of one
    run of the pass: a structural rewrite that dissolves an intermediate keeps its declaration, since
    a caller may still hold the handle, and allocating storage for it spends memory on a tensor whose
    whole point was to stop existing.

    Restricted to graph-owned intermediates on purpose. A caller-owned deferred tensor is one the
    caller may read after the graph runs, so allocating it is deliberate and not a finding.

    A use is counted through aliases, so a tensor written only through a view of it counts as used.

    :param graph: The graph to audit; loop bodies, conditional branches and setup bodies are included.
    :returns: The names, sorted, of every such tensor.


.. py:function:: tile_view(parent: Any, coord: list[int]) -> RuntimeTensorView

    Record a dense, parent-aliasing view of a single tile.

    The tiled capture-view primitive. A tile is a full-rank dense block, so
    the returned ``RuntimeTensorView`` feeds the ENTIRE dense op surface -
    einsum, gemm, permute, axpby, ... - inside capture, which is exactly how
    tiled workflows iterate (per tile block, the shape TiledExpansion itself
    lowers to). Element-granular slicing of a tiled tensor is deliberately
    not offered: a slice crossing tile boundaries has no single buffer.

    The tile is created (deferred, dims fixed by the grid) at capture if
    absent - value-equivalent, since absent tiles are rigorous zeros and a
    created tile materializes zeroed. Each replay the executor re-resolves
    the tile from the LIVE parent and re-emplaces the view, so parents whose
    storage moves between replays (graph-owned deferred scratch released by
    FreeInsertion and re-materialized) stay correct.

    The recorded ViewDescriptor carries one constant ``[c, c+1)`` range per
    axis IN TILE UNITS: the disjointness-aware hazard scan then proves views
    of distinct tiles non-conflicting, so per-tile writes to one output can
    be scheduled concurrently. Units stay consistent because element-unit
    boxes cannot appear on a tiled parent (the dense view entry points do
    not accept one).

    .. code-block:: text

       with cg.capture(g):
             for i in range(2):
                 a = cg.tile_view(A, [i, 0])
                 c = cg.tile_view(C, [i, 0])
                 einsums.einsum("ij <- ik ; kj", c, a, B, c_pf=0.0, ab_pf=1.0)


.. py:function:: tiles(sizes: list[int]) -> SpaceTiling

    A tiled axis with a partition and no space.

    The tiled counterpart of ``fixed``: an axis that is cut up but means nothing chemically,
    so it gets no space and no dim symbol and a bind may not move it.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param sizes: The tile sizes.
    :returns: The axis.


.. py:function:: validate_graph_ir_file(path: str) -> None

    Check ``path``, throwing with every problem when it is not valid.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0

    :param path: The file to check.
    :raises Exception: With one line per problem found.


.. py:function:: view(parent: Any, ranges: list[tuple[int, int]]) -> RuntimeTensorView

    Python-friendly wrapper over ``view_runtime``.

    Takes a list of ``(lo, hi)`` integer pairs, one per parent axis. A pair
    with both values < 0 (the conventional ``(-1, -1)``) means "full axis";
    any other pair becomes a ``[lo, hi)`` range slice.

    Must be called inside a capture context. The returned view is owned by the
    active graph and aliases the parent: writes through it land in the parent,
    and the graph's optimization passes treat it as a dependency on the parent.

    .. code-block:: text

       with cg.capture(g):
             C_occ = einsums.graph.view(C, [(-1, -1), (0, nocc)])  # C[:, :nocc]
             einsums.linalg.gemm(2.0, C_occ, C_occ, 0.0, D, trans_b=True)


.. py:function:: view_indexed(parent: Any, specs: list[tuple[int, int, int]]) -> RuntimeTensorView

    Python-friendly indexed view supporting rank-reducing integer indices.

    One ``(kind, a, b)`` triple per parent axis:

    * ``kind == 0``: full axis (``a``/``b`` ignored)
    * ``kind == 1``: range ``[a, b)``
    * ``kind == 2``: drop, indexing the axis at ``a`` and removing it from the result Result rank = parent rank − (number of drop axes). This is what backs capture-mode rank-reducing reads like ``A[i]`` / ``A[:, j]`` (the plain ``view`` above only does full/range, i.e. rank-preserving slices).


.. py:function:: views(parent: Any, specs: list[list[tuple[int, int, int]]]) -> list[RuntimeTensorView]

    Batched ``view_indexed_python``: record N views of one parent in one call.

    Same per-axis ``(kind, a, b)`` triples as ``view_indexed`` - ``0`` full,
    ``1`` range ``[a, b)``, ``2`` drop at ``a`` - one inner list per view, and
    the views come back in the order they were asked for.

    This exists for callers that build many views of the same parent in a
    loop, which is the shape of every batched capture: slicing per-pair blocks
    out of a pair store issues thousands of these, and the per-call Python and
    pybind overhead - argument normalization, overload resolution, the
    crossing itself - was about a third of what each view cost. One crossing
    for N views leaves only the recording. The recorded graph is identical to
    N ``view_indexed`` calls; nothing downstream can tell the difference.


Enumerations
------------

.. py:class:: ApproximationEffect

    What a lossy pass's tolerance is a bound ON.

    A number without units is not a bound, and the three kinds below do not convert into one
    another: an element-wise bound says nothing about a norm, and a norm bound says nothing
    about the worst element. So a record carries which one it means and composition happens
    within a kind, never across.

    Records of different kinds coexist on one output rather than being refused. There is
    nothing that needs them as a single number: ``Graph::accuracy_spent`` counts one kind at
    a time and ``ApproximationTolerance`` reports the two sides separately, which is the
    honest description of a result that is off by so much in norm and so much per element.
    A declared BUDGET is the exception, because a budget claims to be a cap and can only cap
    its own units; see ``Graph::can_approximate``.

    .. seealso::

       ApproximationRecord

    .. seealso::

       compose_approximation

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0


    .. py:attribute:: ElementWise
        :value: 0

        Worst absolute difference over the elements of the named outputs.


    .. py:attribute:: NormRelative
        :value: 1

        Difference in norm, RELATIVE to the exact result's norm.


    .. py:attribute:: EnergyLike
        :value: 2

        Absolute error in a scalar the outputs reduce to, an energy being the case this
        exists for. Composes with ``ElementWise``, since both are absolute.



.. py:class:: ApproximationOrigin

    Where a bound's number came from.

    A bound and a guess are the same double, and months later a reader of a saved graph cannot
    tell them apart from the number alone. They deserve different trust, so the file carries
    which it is.

    ``Asserted`` is not a lesser answer, it is the only one available wherever measuring would
    mean computing the exact result the approximation exists to avoid computing. A metric fit is
    that case: its error is the difference from the exact tensor, and a caller holding that had
    no reason to fit. ``Measured`` belongs to a provider that genuinely knows, a truncated
    decomposition holding its own discarded singular values being the plain example.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0


    .. py:attribute:: Measured
        :value: 0

        The pass computed the error it actually had.


    .. py:attribute:: Asserted
        :value: 1

        A caller stated the error, and nothing here checked it.



.. py:class:: BisectMode

    How many passes a trial turns on at once.


    .. py:attribute:: Individual
        :value: 0

        One pass per trial. Answers "which pass is wrong on its own", which is the question the
        design states and the one a deterministic recognizer can fail.


    .. py:attribute:: Cumulative
        :value: 1

        Passes one through k. Answers a different question, "which pass is wrong AFTER the ones
        before it", and it is a real one: a pass can be correct alone and wrong on a node set an
        earlier pass rewrote, which is the failure mode a phase with three re-associating members
        invites and which ``BisectMode::Individual`` cannot see.



.. py:class:: ChainPricing

    What ContractionPlanning prices a bracketing against.


    .. py:attribute:: Auto
        :value: 0

        The detected machine's ``CostModel``. The default.


    .. py:attribute:: Flops
        :value: 1

        ``CostModel::flop_count``: flops alone, the same answer on every
        < machine. For tests that must know whether a chain is re-bracketed,
        < which on small shapes the detected model decides by its per-call
        < overheads, and so differently from one machine to the next.



.. py:class:: CrossSpaceSeverity

    How much a ``CrossSpaceFinding`` is worth acting on.


    .. py:attribute:: Note
        :value: 0

        Legitimate, listed for the record: a restriction of one space to a subspace.


    .. py:attribute:: Warning
        :value: 1

        Nothing declared settles whether the two spaces overlap.


    .. py:attribute:: Error
        :value: 2

        The two spaces are declared disjoint, so the contraction is identically zero.



.. py:class:: DecisionKind

    Whether a pass applied something, declined a candidate, or only had something to say.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0


    .. py:attribute:: Applied
        :value: 0

        The pass changed the graph, or chose something it reports.


    .. py:attribute:: Declined
        :value: 1

        The pass looked at a candidate and left it, for a reason.


    .. py:attribute:: Note
        :value: 2

        Something the pass reported that is neither.



.. py:class:: EventKind

    What an ``ExecutionEvent`` reports.

    .. versionadded:: 2.0.0


    .. py:attribute:: ReplayStart
        :value: 0

        The outermost replay began.


    .. py:attribute:: NodeStart
        :value: 1

        A node began, on the thread that runs it.


    .. py:attribute:: NodeEnd
        :value: 2

        That node finished, or threw.


    .. py:attribute:: Read
        :value: 3

        A slab, box or whole tensor was read from a backing's storage.


    .. py:attribute:: Write
        :value: 4

        One was written to it.


    .. py:attribute:: Publish
        :value: 5

        An update backing's new value was made current.


    .. py:attribute:: ReplayEnd
        :value: 6

        The outermost replay finished.



.. py:class:: Factor

    Whether to let the cost model decide that a group is worth factoring.


    .. py:attribute:: Auto
        :value: 0

        Price the trade against the ``CostModel``. The default.


    .. py:attribute:: Always
        :value: 1

        Factor whenever the rewrite is structurally possible, whatever it
        < costs. For tests that exercise the rewrite on shapes too small to
        < be worth it, and for callers who know their workload better than
        < the model does.



.. py:class:: OpKind

    Identifies the kind of operation a node represents.

    Used by optimization passes for pattern matching. For example,
    ScaleAbsorption looks for OpKind::Scale followed by a prefactor-bearing op.

    Categories:

    - **TensorAlgebra**: Einsum, Permute, Transpose, ElementTransform, KhatriRao
    - **BLAS-level**: Gemm, Gemv, Ger, Dot, Scale, Axpby, DirectProduct
    - **LAPACK-level**: SVD, QR, Syev, Heev, Geev, Gesv, Getrf, Getrs, Invert, Det, Pow, etc.
    - **Other**: HPTTPermute, Custom


    .. py:attribute:: Einsum
        :value: 0

        Tensor contraction from a string spec, via dispatch::string_einsum()


    .. py:attribute:: Permute
        :value: 1

        Index reordering from a string spec, via dispatch::string_permute_impl()


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedPermute
        :value: 2

        Many independent index reorderings, one per member


    .. py:attribute:: Transpose
        :value: 3

        2D transpose, via tensor_permute::transpose()


    .. py:attribute:: ElementTransform
        :value: 4

        Element-wise unary transform


    .. py:attribute:: KhatriRao
        :value: 5

        Khatri-Rao product


    .. py:attribute:: BatchedGemm
        :value: 6

        Many independent GEMMs in one `gemm_batch` call


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedBatchedGemm
        :value: 7

        Many independent GEMMs of DIFFERING shape in one `gemm_batch_grouped` call


    .. py:attribute:: Gemm
        :value: 8

        General matrix-matrix multiply (BLAS Level 3)


    .. py:attribute:: Gemv
        :value: 9

        General matrix-vector multiply (BLAS Level 2)


    .. py:attribute:: Ger
        :value: 10

        Rank-1 update (BLAS Level 2)


    .. py:attribute:: Dot
        :value: 11

        Dot product (returns scalar)


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedDot
        :value: 12

        Many independent dot products, one per entry, in one node


    .. py:attribute:: Scale
        :value: 13

        Scalar multiplication of entire tensor


    .. py:attribute:: Axpby
        :value: 14

        Y = alpha * X + beta * Y


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedAxpby
        :value: 15

        Many independent `Y = alpha*X + beta*Y`, one per entry, in one node


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedSandwich
        :value: 16

        Many independent q-tiled dressed sandwich accumulations, one per entry


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedGatherRotate
        :value: 17

        Many independent q-tiled gather-and-rotate blocks, one per entry


    .. py:attribute:: DirectProduct
        :value: 18

        Element-wise (Hadamard) product


    .. py:attribute:: DirectDivision
        :value: 19

        Element-wise (Hadamard) quotient


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedDirectProduct
        :value: 20

        Many independent element-wise products, one per member


    .. py:attribute:: GroupedDirectDivision
        :value: 21

        Many independent element-wise quotients, one per member


    .. py:attribute:: SVD
        :value: 22

        Singular value decomposition


    .. py:attribute:: SVD_DD
        :value: 23

        SVD with divide-and-conquer algorithm


    .. py:attribute:: TruncatedSVD
        :value: 24

        Truncated SVD (keeping k singular values)


    .. py:attribute:: QR
        :value: 25

        QR decomposition


    .. py:attribute:: Syev
        :value: 26

        Symmetric eigendecomposition


    .. py:attribute:: Heev
        :value: 27

        Hermitian eigendecomposition


    .. py:attribute:: Geev
        :value: 28

        General eigendecomposition


    .. py:attribute:: TruncatedSyev
        :value: 29

        Truncated symmetric eigendecomposition


    .. py:attribute:: Gesv
        :value: 30

        General linear system solver (AX = B)


    .. py:attribute:: Getrf
        :value: 31

        LU factorization


    .. py:attribute:: Getrs
        :value: 32

        Solve against an LU factorization


    .. py:attribute:: Getri
        :value: 33

        Inverse from LU factorization


    .. py:attribute:: Invert
        :value: 34

        Matrix inverse


    .. py:attribute:: Pseudoinverse
        :value: 35

        Moore-Penrose pseudoinverse


    .. py:attribute:: Det
        :value: 36

        Matrix determinant (returns scalar)


    .. py:attribute:: Pow
        :value: 37

        Matrix power


    .. py:attribute:: SymmGemm
        :value: 38

        Symmetric double multiply: C = B^T * A * B


    .. py:attribute:: Norm
        :value: 39

        Tensor norm (returns scalar)


    .. py:attribute:: SolveLyapunov
        :value: 40

        Continuous Lyapunov equation solver


    .. py:attribute:: HPTTPermute
        :value: 41

        High-performance tensor transpose


    .. py:attribute:: Conditional
        :value: 42

        If-then-else branch with subgraphs


    .. py:attribute:: Loop
        :value: 43

        While/for loop with body subgraph


    .. py:attribute:: Setup
        :value: 44

        Body computed once per bound problem, skipped by later replays


    .. py:attribute:: LaplaceQuadrature
        :value: 45

        Laplace-transform points, weights and per-axis exponentials, refit per bind


    .. py:attribute:: Alloc
        :value: 46

        Tensor allocation (marks lifetime start)


    .. py:attribute:: Free
        :value: 47

        Tensor deallocation (marks lifetime end)


    .. py:attribute:: HostToDevice
        :value: 48

        Transfer tensor from host to device memory


    .. py:attribute:: DeviceToHost
        :value: 49

        Transfer tensor from device to host memory


    .. py:attribute:: ParallelFor
        :value: 50

        Data-parallel for loop (delegates to TaskPool)


    .. py:attribute:: ParallelReduce
        :value: 51

        Data-parallel reduce (delegates to TaskPool)


    .. py:attribute:: DiskRead
        :value: 52

        Read tensor data from disk (HDF5, binary, etc.)


    .. py:attribute:: DiskWrite
        :value: 53

        Write tensor data to disk (checkpointing)


    .. py:attribute:: Materialize
        :value: 54

        Allocate storage for a deferred (shell) tensor


    .. py:attribute:: Initialize
        :value: 55

        Fill tensor with initial values (zero, random, disk)


    .. py:attribute:: View
        :value: 56

        Non-owning slice/view of another tensor (zero-copy alias)


    .. py:attribute:: WriteParam
        :value: 57

        Write the value of a scalar tensor into a Pipeline parameter


    .. py:attribute:: Trace
        :value: 58

        Diagonal sum of a square rank-2 tensor (returns scalar)


    .. py:attribute:: Allreduce
        :value: 59

        Sum partial results across MPI ranks


    .. py:attribute:: Broadcast
        :value: 60

        Root sends data to all ranks


    .. py:attribute:: Allgather
        :value: 61

        Each rank contributes a piece, all receive the whole


    .. py:attribute:: Scatter
        :value: 62

        Root distributes pieces to ranks


    .. py:attribute:: Barrier
        :value: 63

        Synchronization point across ranks


    .. py:attribute:: TileGather
        :value: 64

        Copy a tiled tensor's tiles into one dense buffer


    .. py:attribute:: TileScatter
        :value: 65

        Copy a dense buffer back into a tiled tensor's tiles


    .. py:attribute:: TileElementwise
        :value: 66

        Apply one elementwise operation to a whole list of tiles


    .. py:attribute:: DiisStep
        :value: 67

        Pulay DIIS extrapolation over an accelerator's (amplitude, step) pairs


    .. py:attribute:: Custom
        :value: 68

        User-registered custom operation



.. py:class:: Tristate

    Answer to a relation query that may simply not be known.

    The registry knows only what was declared and what follows; a rewriting pass treats Unknown as No.


    .. py:attribute:: No
        :value: 0

        The relation is known not to hold.


    .. py:attribute:: Yes
        :value: 1

        The relation is known to hold.


    .. py:attribute:: Unknown
        :value: 2

        Nothing declared or derivable settles the question.
